```java
            CompletableFuture.completedFuture(new HashMap<>());
        
        for (CompletableFuture<Map<String, Long>> future : mapFutures) {
            result = result.thenCombine(future, this::mergeMaps);
        }
        
        return result;
    }
    
    private Map<String, Long> processFile(Path file) {
        // Simulate file processing
        Map<String, Long> counts = new HashMap<>();
        counts.put("word" + file.hashCode(), 1L);
        return counts;
    }
    
    private Map<String, Long> mergeMaps(
            Map<String, Long> m1, Map<String, Long> m2) {
        Map<String, Long> result = new HashMap<>(m1);
        m2.forEach((k, v) -> result.merge(k, v, Long::sum));
        return result;
    }
}
```

**When to Use MapReduce Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. When processing extremely large datasets (terabytes+).

2. When the problem can be expressed as map and reduce operations.

3. When you need fault tolerance and distributed processing.

4. When you have many machines available.

5. For batch processing, not real-time.

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Search Engines:** Building inverted indexes
- **Log Analysis:** Processing server logs
- **Data Mining:** Finding patterns in large datasets
- **Machine Learning:** Training data preparation
- **ETL Pipelines:** Extract, transform, load operations

**Advantages of MapReduce Pattern**

1. **Scalability:** Can scale to thousands of machines.

2. **Fault Tolerance:** Tasks can be retried on failure.

3. **Simplicity:** Programmers only write map and reduce functions.

4. **Data Locality:** Moves computation to data.

5. **Load Balancing:** Automatically handles stragglers.

**Disadvantages of MapReduce Pattern**

1. **Batch Orientation:** Not for real-time processing.

2. **Rigid Structure:** Only map and reduce phases.

3. **Disk I/O Heavy:** Intermediate data written to disk.

4. **Latency:** High latency due to shuffling.

5. **Complexity:** Distributed systems are complex.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| MapReduce | Large-scale distributed | Designed for clusters |
| Fork-Join | In-memory parallel | Single JVM |
| Fan-Out/Fan-In | Parallel independent tasks | Simpler, no shuffle |
| Pipeline | Sequential stages | Linear flow |

**MapReduce vs Fork-Join**

- **MapReduce:** Distributed, disk-based, fault-tolerant
- **Fork-Join:** In-memory, single JVM, no fault tolerance

**MapReduce vs Spark**

- **MapReduce:** Writes intermediate results to disk
- **Spark:** Keeps data in memory (faster for iterative algorithms)

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Too Many Small Files:** Overheads of many map tasks.

2. **Skewed Data:** Some reducers get much more data.

3. **Stragglers:** Slow tasks delay entire job.

4. **Resource Contention:** Competing for cluster resources.

5. **Debugging Difficulty:** Hard to debug distributed jobs.

**Best Practices**

1. **Use Combiners:** Reduce data shuffled.

2. **Balance Partitions:** Ensure even distribution.

3. **Handle Stragglers:** Speculative execution.

4. **Monitor Jobs:** Track progress and resource usage.

5. **Test with Sample Data:** Verify logic on smaller datasets.

6. **Consider Alternatives:** Use Spark for iterative algorithms.

---

[Page 26]

# PART FIVE: EVENT HANDLING PATTERNS

## Chapter 24: Reactor Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Reactor pattern handles service requests delivered concurrently to an application by one or more clients. It demultiplexes incoming requests and dispatches them synchronously to associated request handlers.

**Understanding the Problem**

In network servers, you need to handle multiple connections simultaneously. Traditional approaches:

- **Thread-per-connection:** Creates many threads, not scalable
- **Thread pool:** Still has context switching overhead
- **Polling:** Wastes CPU cycles

The challenge is to handle many connections efficiently with limited threads.

**The Solution**

The Reactor pattern uses:

1. **Event Demultiplexer:** Waits for events on multiple handles (select/poll/epoll)
2. **Reactor:** Demultiplexes events and dispatches to handlers
3. **Handlers:** Process specific events

**Key Concepts**

- **Handle:** Resource (socket, file descriptor) that can generate events
- **Synchronous Event Demultiplexer:** select(), poll(), epoll()
- **Reactor:** Demultiplexes and dispatches events
- **Event Handler:** Processes specific event types
- **Initiation Dispatcher:** Registers handlers and dispatches events

**Diagram: Reactor Pattern**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                       REACTOR PATTERN                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                    COMPONENTS                          │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │               REACTOR                         │   │    │
│  │  │  (Initiation Dispatcher)                     │   │    │

│  │  │  • Demultiplexes events                       │   │    │
│  │  │  • Dispatches to handlers                     │   │    │
```

**Implementation Example**

```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.net.InetSocketAddress;
import java.nio.ByteBuffer;
import java.nio.channels.*;
import java.util.Iterator;
import java.util.Set;
import java.util.concurrent.*;

// 1. Event Handler Interface
interface EventHandler {
    void handleEvent(SelectionKey key) throws IOException;
}

// 2. Concrete Handlers
class AcceptHandler implements EventHandler {
    private final ServerSocketChannel serverChannel;
    
    public AcceptHandler(ServerSocketChannel serverChannel) {
        this.serverChannel = serverChannel;
    }
    
    @Override
    public void handleEvent(SelectionKey key) throws IOException {
        SocketChannel clientChannel = serverChannel.accept();
        clientChannel.configureBlocking(false);
        
        // Register read interest for new connection
        clientChannel.register(key.selector(), SelectionKey.OP_READ, 
            new ReadHandler());
        
        System.out.println("Accepted connection from: " + 
            clientChannel.getRemoteAddress());
    }
}

class ReadHandler implements EventHandler {
    private final ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1024);
    
    @Override
    public void handleEvent(SelectionKey key) throws IOException {
        SocketChannel clientChannel = (SocketChannel) key.channel();
        
        int bytesRead = clientChannel.read(buffer);
        
        if (bytesRead == -1) {
            clientChannel.close();
            System.out.println("Connection closed");
            return;
        }
        
        if (bytesRead > 0) {
            buffer.flip();
            byte[] data = new byte[buffer.limit()];
            buffer.get(data);
            String message = new String(data);
            System.out.println("Received: " + message);
            
            // Echo back
            buffer.rewind();
            clientChannel.write(buffer);
            buffer.clear();
        }
    }
}

// 3. Reactor (Initiation Dispatcher)
class Reactor {
    private final Selector selector;
    private final ExecutorService workerPool;
    private volatile boolean running = true;
    
    public Reactor() throws IOException {
        this.selector = Selector.open();
        this.workerPool = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(10);
    }
    
    public void registerChannel(SelectableChannel channel, int ops, 
                                EventHandler handler) throws IOException {
        channel.configureBlocking(false);
        channel.register(selector, ops, handler);
    }
    
    public void run() {
        try {
            while (running) {
                // Wait for events (synchronous event demultiplexing)
                selector.select();
                
                // Get ready events
                Set<SelectionKey> selectedKeys = selector.selectedKeys();
                Iterator<SelectionKey> iter = selectedKeys.iterator();
                
                while (iter.hasNext()) {
                    SelectionKey key = iter.next();
                    iter.remove();
                    
                    // Dispatch event to handler
                    if (key.isValid()) {
                        EventHandler handler = (EventHandler) key.attachment();
                        
                        // Handle in worker thread for long operations
                        if (key.isAcceptable() || key.isReadable()) {
                            workerPool.submit(() -> {
                                try {
                                    handler.handleEvent(key);
                                } catch (IOException e) {
                                    e.printStackTrace();
                                }
                            });
                        } else {
                            handler.handleEvent(key);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        } catch (IOException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
    
    public void shutdown() {
        running = false;
        workerPool.shutdown();
        try {
            selector.close();
        } catch (IOException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
}

// 4. Server Example
public class ReactorServer {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        Reactor reactor = new Reactor();
        
        // Create server socket channel
        ServerSocketChannel serverChannel = ServerSocketChannel.open();
        serverChannel.bind(new InetSocketAddress(8080));
        
        // Register with reactor for accept events
        reactor.registerChannel(serverChannel, SelectionKey.OP_ACCEPT, 
            new AcceptHandler(serverChannel));
        
        System.out.println("Reactor server started on port 8080");
        
        // Run reactor in separate thread
        new Thread(() -> reactor.run()).start();
        
        // Shutdown hook
        Runtime.getRuntime().addShutdownHook(new Thread(() -> {
            reactor.shutdown();
            System.out.println("Server stopped");
        }));
    }
}
```

**Simplified Reactor with Single Thread**

```java
class SimpleReactor {
    private final Selector selector;
    private final Map<SelectionKey, Handler> handlers = new HashMap<>();
    
    public SimpleReactor() throws IOException {
        this.selector = Selector.open();
    }
    
    public void registerChannel(SelectableChannel channel, int ops, 
                                Handler handler) throws IOException {
        channel.configureBlocking(false);
        SelectionKey key = channel.register(selector, ops);
        handlers.put(key, handler);
    }
    
    public void run() throws IOException {
        while (true) {
            selector.select();
            Iterator<SelectionKey> iter = selector.selectedKeys().iterator();
            
            while (iter.hasNext()) {
                SelectionKey key = iter.next();
                iter.remove();
                
                Handler handler = handlers.get(key);
                if (handler != null) {
                    handler.handle(key);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    
    interface Handler {
        void handle(SelectionKey key) throws IOException;
    }
}
```

**When to Use Reactor Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. When handling many concurrent connections.

2. When connections are mostly idle (waiting for I/O).

3. When you need high scalability.

4. In network servers (HTTP, WebSocket, etc.).

5. When you want to minimize thread usage.

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Web Servers:** Nginx, Node.js (uses event loop similar to reactor)
- **Database Drivers:** Asynchronous database clients
- **Message Queues:** RabbitMQ, Kafka
- **Proxy Servers:** Handling many connections
- **Chat Servers:** Managing many client connections

**Advantages of Reactor Pattern**

1. **Scalability:** Handles thousands of connections with few threads.

2. **Efficiency:** No thread per connection overhead.

3. **Resource Usage:** Minimal threads.

4. **Performance:** Good for I/O-bound applications.

5. **Separation of Concerns:** Event handling separated from demultiplexing.

**Disadvantages of Reactor Pattern**

1. **Complexity:** More complex than thread-per-connection.

2. **Debugging Difficulty:** Event flow harder to trace.

3. **Blocking Operations:** Blocking in handler blocks reactor.

4. **CPU-Bound Tasks:** Not suitable for CPU-intensive work.

5. **Ordering Guarantees:** Events may be processed out of order.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| Reactor | Sync event demux | Single-threaded demux |
| Proactor | Async event demux | Uses async I/O |
| Leader-Followers | Thread pool variant | Different thread model |
| Event Loop | Similar concept | Often single-threaded |

**Reactor vs Proactor**

- **Reactor:** Synchronous I/O, notifies when ready
- **Proactor:** Asynchronous I/O, notifies when complete

**Reactor vs Event Loop**

- **Reactor:** Formal pattern with handlers
- **Event Loop:** Simpler, often used in JavaScript

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Blocking Handlers:** Blocking in handler blocks reactor.

2. **Resource Leaks:** Not closing channels properly.

3. **Callback Hell:** Complex nested callbacks.

4. **Thread Safety:** Handlers may need synchronization.

5. **Error Handling:** Errors can leave reactor in bad state.

**Best Practices**

1. **Keep Handlers Non-blocking:** Use worker threads for long operations.

2. **Handle Errors Gracefully:** Don't crash reactor on handler errors.

3. **Use Timeouts:** Prevent hanging connections.

4. **Monitor Selector:** Watch for large select sets.

5. **Consider Hybrid Approaches:** Combine with thread pool for CPU work.

6. **Test with Many Connections:** Verify scalability.

---

[Page 27]

## Chapter 25: Proactor Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Proactor pattern handles asynchronous events by initiating asynchronous operations and then being notified upon their completion. It's the asynchronous counterpart to the Reactor pattern.

**Understanding the Problem**

While the Reactor pattern efficiently handles many connections, it still uses synchronous I/O operations. When a read operation is initiated, the thread must wait for data. This leads to:

1. **Thread Blocking:** Threads block on I/O
2. **Context Switching:** Switching between threads
3. **Complexity:** Managing read states

**The Solution**

The Proactor pattern uses asynchronous I/O operations:

1. **Initiate Operation:** Start async read/write
2. **Continue Processing:** Thread doesn't block
3. **Completion Notification:** OS notifies when operation completes
4. **Completion Handler:** Processes completed operation

**Key Concepts**

- **Asynchronous Operation:** I/O operation that completes in background
- **Completion Handler:** Processes operation results
- **Proactor:** Initiates operations and dispatches completions
- **Completion Port:** Windows IOCP, Linux AIO, epoll with EPOLLET

**Diagram: Proactor Pattern**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                       PROACTOR PATTERN                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                    COMPONENTS                          │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │              PROACTOR                         │   │    │
│  │  │  • Initiates async operations                 │   │    │
│  │  │  • Waits for completions                      │   │    │
│  │  │  • Dispatches to handlers                     │   │    │
│  │  └─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘   │    │
│  │                        │                             │    │
│  │                        │ initiates                   │    │
│  │                        ▼                             │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │     ASYNCHRONOUS OPERATION                    │   │    │
│  │  │  • Async read/write                           │   │    │
│  │  │  • Runs in background                          │   │    │
│  │  └─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘   │    │
│  │                        │                             │    │
│  │                        │ completes                   │    │
│  │                        ▼                             │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │         COMPLETION HANDLERS                   │   │    │
│  │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐   │   │    │
│  │  │  │Read      │  │Write     │  │Accept    │   │   │    │
│  │  │  │Handler   │  │Handler   │  │Handler   │   │   │    │
│  │  │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────┘   │   │    │
│  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                   SEQUENCE DIAGRAM                     │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Proactor    Handler1    OS         AsyncOp    Handler2  │
│  │    │            │        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │──read_async─>│       │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │──submit─>│           │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │<─ok──────│           │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │──completes─>│           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │<─data───────│           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │<─complete──│        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │──handle─>   │        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │──process─>│           │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │            │           │     │    │
│  │    │            │        │            │           │     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │            REACTOR VS PROACTOR                         │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Reactor:                                         │    │
│  │    select() → ready → read → process               │    │
│  │         └── synchronous read ──┘                   │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Proactor:                                         │    │
│  │    async_read() → process other work → completion  │    │
│  │         └── background read ──┘                    │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Proactor generally requires OS support            │    │
│  │  (IOCP on Windows, AIO on Linux)                   │    │
│  │                                                     │    │

│                                                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Implementation Example (using Java AIO - NIO.2)**

```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.net.InetSocketAddress;
import java.nio.ByteBuffer;
import java.nio.channels.*;
import java.util.concurrent.*;

// 1. Completion Handler Interface
interface CompletionHandler<V, A> {
    void completed(V result, A attachment);
    void failed(Throwable exc, A attachment);
}

// 2. Proactor Server
class ProactorServer {
    private final AsynchronousServerSocketChannel serverChannel;
    private final ExecutorService workerPool;
    
    public ProactorServer(int port) throws IOException {
        this.serverChannel = AsynchronousServerSocketChannel.open();
        this.serverChannel.bind(new InetSocketAddress(port));
        this.workerPool = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(10);
    }
    
    public void start() {
        System.out.println("Proactor server started on port " + 
            serverChannel.getLocalAddress());
        
        // Start accepting connections
        acceptNext();
        
        // Keep main thread alive
        try {
            Thread.currentThread().join();
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        }
    }
    
    private void acceptNext() {
        serverChannel.accept(null, new CompletionHandler<AsynchronousSocketChannel, Void>() {
            @Override
            public void completed(AsynchronousSocketChannel clientChannel, Void attachment) {
                // Accept next connection immediately
                acceptNext();
                
                // Handle this connection
                handleConnection(clientChannel);
            }
            
            @Override
            public void failed(Throwable exc, Void attachment) {
                System.err.println("Accept failed: " + exc.getMessage());
                // Try again after delay
                workerPool.schedule(() -> acceptNext(), 1, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
            }
        });
    }
    
    private void handleConnection(AsynchronousSocketChannel clientChannel) {
        try {
            System.out.println("New connection from: " + 
                clientChannel.getRemoteAddress());
        } catch (IOException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
        
        // Create buffer for this connection
        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1024);
        
        // Start async read
        readAsync(clientChannel, buffer);
    }
    
    private void readAsync(AsynchronousSocketChannel channel, ByteBuffer buffer) {
        channel.read(buffer, buffer, new CompletionHandler<Integer, ByteBuffer>() {
            @Override
            public void completed(Integer bytesRead, ByteBuffer attachment) {
                if (bytesRead == -1) {
                    // Connection closed
                    try {
                        channel.close();
                        System.out.println("Connection closed");
                    } catch (IOException e) {
                        e.printStackTrace();
                    }
                    return;
                }
                
                if (bytesRead > 0) {
                    attachment.flip();
                    byte[] data = new byte[attachment.limit()];
                    attachment.get(data);
                    String message = new String(data);
                    System.out.println("Received: " + message);
                    
                    // Echo back
                    attachment.rewind();
                    writeAsync(channel, attachment);
                }
                
                // Read next message
                attachment.clear();
                readAsync(channel, attachment);
            }
            
            @Override
            public void failed(Throwable exc, ByteBuffer attachment) {
                System.err.println("Read failed: " + exc.getMessage());
                try {
                    channel.close();
                } catch (IOException e) {
                    e.printStackTrace();
                }
            }
        });
    }
    
    private void writeAsync(AsynchronousSocketChannel channel, ByteBuffer buffer) {
        channel.write(buffer, buffer, new CompletionHandler<Integer, ByteBuffer>() {
            @Override
            public void completed(Integer bytesWritten, ByteBuffer attachment) {
                if (attachment.hasRemaining()) {
                    // Write remaining data
                    channel.write(attachment, attachment, this);
                }
            }
            
            @Override
            public void failed(Throwable exc, ByteBuffer attachment) {
                System.err.println("Write failed: " + exc.getMessage());
                try {
                    channel.close();
                } catch (IOException e) {
                    e.printStackTrace();
                }
            }
        });
    }
    
    public void shutdown() {
        try {
            serverChannel.close();
        } catch (IOException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
        workerPool.shutdown();
    }
    
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        ProactorServer server = new ProactorServer(8080);
        Runtime.getRuntime().addShutdownHook(new Thread(server::shutdown));
        server.start();
    }
}
```

**Simplified Proactor with Futures**

```java
class SimpleProactor {
    private final AsynchronousChannelGroup channelGroup;
    
    public SimpleProactor() throws IOException {
        this.channelGroup = AsynchronousChannelGroup.withFixedThreadPool(
            10, Executors.defaultThreadFactory());
    }
    
    public Future<Integer> readAsync(AsynchronousSocketChannel channel, 
                                     ByteBuffer buffer) {
        return channel.read(buffer);
    }
    
    public Future<Integer> writeAsync(AsynchronousSocketChannel channel, 
                                      ByteBuffer buffer) {
        return channel.write(buffer);
    }
    
    public void processWithFuture(AsynchronousSocketChannel channel) 
            throws Exception {
        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1024);
        
        // Start async read
        Future<Integer> readFuture = readAsync(channel, buffer);
        
        // Do other work while reading
        System.out.println("Reading in background...");
        
        // Wait for read to complete
        Integer bytesRead = readFuture.get(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
        
        if (bytesRead > 0) {
            buffer.flip();
            System.out.println("Read " + bytesRead + " bytes");
            
            // Echo back
            Future<Integer> writeFuture = writeAsync(channel, buffer);
            writeFuture.get(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
        }
    }
}
```

**When to Use Proactor Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. When OS supports true asynchronous I/O (IOCP on Windows, AIO on Linux).

2. When you want maximum I/O scalability.

3. When you have many long-running I/O operations.

4. When you want to minimize thread usage.

5. In high-performance servers handling many concurrent connections.

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Windows Servers:** Using IOCP (I/O Completion Ports)
- **Database Systems:** Asynchronous query execution
- **File Servers:** Handling many file transfers
- **Proxy Servers:** Forwarding data between connections
- **High-Performance Computing:** Overlapping computation with I/O

**Advantages of Proactor Pattern**

1. **Maximum Concurrency:** Threads never block on I/O.

2. **Scalability:** Handles huge numbers of connections.

3. **Efficiency:** No thread blocking or context switching.

4. **Simplified Code:** No need to manage read states.

5. **Overlap:** Computation and I/O can overlap.

**Disadvantages of Proactor Pattern**

1. **OS Support:** Requires OS-level async I/O.

2. **Complexity:** More complex than reactor.

3. **Debugging:** Asynchronous flow harder to debug.

4. **Error Handling:** Errors reported asynchronously.

5. **Memory Management:** Buffers must stay alive until completion.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| Proactor | Async I/O demux | Notifies on completion |
| Reactor | Sync I/O demux | Notifies on readiness |
| Event Loop | Simple async | Usually sync I/O |
| AIO | OS async I/O | Underlying mechanism |

**Proactor vs Reactor**

- **Proactor:** "Tell me when you're done"
- **Reactor:** "Tell me when you're ready"

**Proactor vs AIO**

- **Proactor:** Design pattern using AIO
- **AIO:** Operating system asynchronous I/O facility

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Buffer Management:** Buffers must stay valid until completion.

2. **Completion Order:** Completions may come out of order.

3. **Resource Leaks:** Not releasing buffers after completion.

4. **Callback Hell:** Nested completion handlers.

5. **Thread Safety:** Completion handlers may run on arbitrary threads.

**Best Practices**

1. **Pool Buffers:** Reuse buffers to avoid allocation overhead.

2. **Handle Errors:** Always provide failure handlers.

3. **Limit Concurrency:** Don't initiate too many operations.

4. **Monitor Completion Queue:** Watch for backlog.

5. **Use Timeouts:** Prevent hanging operations.

6. **Consider Hybrid:** Combine with thread pool for CPU work.

---

[Page 28]

## Chapter 26: Event Loop Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Event Loop pattern continuously waits for and dispatches events in a single thread. It's the foundation of many asynchronous systems, particularly in JavaScript/Node.js and GUI applications.

**Understanding the Problem**

Traditional threaded approaches have overhead:

1. **Thread Creation:** Expensive
2. **Context Switching:** CPU overhead
3. **Synchronization:** Complex locking
4. **Memory:** Each thread needs stack

For I/O-bound applications, much time is spent waiting. Why have many threads waiting when one thread can handle all waiting?

**The Solution**

The Event Loop pattern uses a single thread that:

1. **Waits** for events (I/O, timers, callbacks)
2. **Processes** events one at a time
3. **Queues** new events during processing
4. **Repeats** forever

**Key Concepts**

- **Event Queue:** Holds pending events
- **Event Loop:** Continuously checks queue and processes events
- **Callbacks:** Functions to execute when events occur
- **Non-blocking I/O:** Operations don't block the loop
- **Microtask Queue:** High-priority queue for promises

**Diagram: Event Loop**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      EVENT LOOP PATTERN                        │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                   EVENT LOOP                           │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
│  │  │              EVENT QUEUE                       │  │    │
│  │  │  ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐            │  │    │
│  │  │  │Event│ │Event│ │Event│ │Event│            │  │    │
│  │  │  │  1  │ │  2  │ │  3  │ │  4  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  └─────┘ └─────┘ └─────┘ └─────┘            │  │    │
│  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
│  │                        │                            │    │
│  │                        │ dequeue                    │    │
│  │                        ▼                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
│  │  │              PROCESS EVENT                      │  │    │
│  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │    │
│  │  │  │ while (queue not empty) {              │  │  │    │
│  │  │  │   event = queue.dequeue()              │  │  │    │
│  │  │  │   event.callback()                     │  │  │    │
│  │  │  │ }                                      │  │  │    │
│  │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │    │
│  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
│  │                        │                            │    │
│  │                        │ new events                 │    │
│  │                        ▼                            │    │
│  │              ┌───────────────────┐                  │    │
│  │              │   Timer expires   │                  │    │
│  │              │   I/O completes   │                  │    │
│  │              │   User interaction│                  │    │
│  │              └───────────────────┘                  │    │
│  │                        │                            │    │
│  │                        │ enqueue                     │    │
│  │                        └─────────────┐              │    │
│  │                                      │              │    │
│  │                                      ▼              │    │
│  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
│  │  │            MICROTASK QUEUE                     │  │    │
│  │  │  (Promise callbacks, nextTick)                │  │    │
│  │  │  ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐                             │  │    │
│  │  │  │Task │ │Task │                             │  │    │
│  │  │  │  1  │ │  2  │                             │  │    │
│  │  │  └─────┘ └─────┘                             │  │    │
│  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
│  │                        │                            │    │
│  │                        │ processed before next event│    │
│  │                        ▼                            │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                EVENT LOOP PHASES                       │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │ timers   │  (setTimeout, setInterval)           │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │ I/O      │  (callbacks from I/O)                │    │
│  │  │ callbacks│                                      │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │ idle,    │  (internal use)                      │    │
│  │  │ prepare  │                                      │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │   poll   │  (retrieve new I/O events)          │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │   check  │  (setImmediate)                      │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │   close  │  (close callbacks)                   │    │
│  │  │ callbacks│                                      │    │
│  │  └──────────┘                                      │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Implementation Examples**

**1. JavaScript/Node.js Event Loop**

```javascript
// JavaScript's event loop is built-in
console.log('Start');

setTimeout(() => {
    console.log('Timeout 1');
}, 0);

Promise.resolve().then(() => {
    console.log('Promise 1');
});

setTimeout(() => {
    console.log('Timeout 2');
}, 0);

console.log('End');

// Output:
// Start
// End
// Promise 1
// Timeout 1
// Timeout 2
```

**2. Simple Event Loop in Java**

```java
import java.util.concurrent.*;
import java.util.*;

public class SimpleEventLoop {
    private final BlockingQueue<Runnable> taskQueue = 
        new LinkedBlockingQueue<>();
    private final BlockingQueue<Runnable> microTaskQueue = 
        new LinkedBlockingQueue<>();
    private volatile boolean running = true;
    
    public void post(Runnable task) {
        taskQueue.offer(task);
    }
    
    public void postMicro(Runnable task) {
        microTaskQueue.offer(task);
    }
    
    public void run() {
        while (running) {
            try {
                // Process all microtasks first
                while (!microTaskQueue.isEmpty()) {
                    Runnable microTask = microTaskQueue.poll();
                    if (microTask != null) {
                        microTask.run();
                    }
                }
                
                // Take next task (blocks if none)
                Runnable task = taskQueue.poll(100, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);
                if (task != null) {
                    task.run();
                }
                
                // Check timers, I/O, etc. (simplified)
                checkTimers();
                
            } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    
    public void stop() {
        running = false;
    }
    
    private void checkTimers() {
        // Check for expired timers and add them to queue
        // Simplified - actual implementation would track timers
    }
    
    // Timer support
    private final Map<Long, Runnable> timers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final ScheduledExecutorService timerExecutor = 
        Executors.newSingleThreadScheduledExecutor();
    
    public void setTimeout(Runnable task, long delay, TimeUnit unit) {
        long id = System.nanoTime();
        timers.put(id, task);
        
        timerExecutor.schedule(() -> {
            timers.remove(id);
            post(task);
        }, delay, unit);
    }
    
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SimpleEventLoop eventLoop = new SimpleEventLoop();
        
        // Start event loop in separate thread
        Thread loopThread = new Thread(() -> eventLoop.run());
        loopThread.start();
        
        // Post some tasks
        eventLoop.post(() -> System.out.println("Task 1"));
        eventLoop.postMicro(() -> System.out.println("Microtask 1"));
        eventLoop.post(() -> System.out.println("Task 2"));
        
        eventLoop.setTimeout(() -> 
            System.out.println("Timeout"), 1, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
        
        // Let it run for a while
        try {
            Thread.sleep(2000);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        }
        
        eventLoop.stop();
        loopThread.interrupt();
    }
}
```

**3. Generic Event Loop with Handlers**

```java
import java.util.*;
import java.util.concurrent.*;

interface Event {
    String getType();
    Object getData();
}

interface EventHandler {
    void handle(Event event);
}

class EventLoop {
    private final BlockingQueue<Event> eventQueue = 
        new LinkedBlockingQueue<>();
    private final Map<String, List<EventHandler>> handlers = 
        new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final Map<Long, Event> timerEvents = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private volatile boolean running = true;
    
    public void registerHandler(String eventType, EventHandler handler) {
        handlers.computeIfAbsent(eventType, k -> new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>())
                .add(handler);
    }
    
    public void post(Event event) {
        eventQueue.offer(event);
    }
    
    public void postDelayed(Event event, long delay, TimeUnit unit) {
        long id = System.nanoTime();
        timerEvents.put(id, event);
        
        ScheduledExecutorService executor = Executors.newSingleThreadScheduledExecutor();
        executor.schedule(() -> {
            timerEvents.remove(id);
            post(event);
            executor.shutdown();
        }, delay, unit);
    }
    
    public void run() {
        while (running) {
            try {
                Event event = eventQueue.poll(100, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);
                if (event != null) {
                    dispatch(event);
                }
            } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    
    private void dispatch(Event event) {
        List<EventHandler> eventHandlers = handlers.get(event.getType());
        if (eventHandlers != null) {
            for (EventHandler handler : eventHandlers) {
                handler.handle(event);
            }
        }
    }
    
    public void stop() {
        running = false;
    }
}

// Example usage
class EventLoopExample {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        EventLoop loop = new EventLoop();
        
        // Register handlers
        loop.registerHandler("USER_LOGIN", event -> {
            System.out.println("User logged in: " + event.getData());
        });
        
        loop.registerHandler("DATA_UPDATE", event -> {
            System.out.println("Data updated: " + event.getData());
        });
        
        // Start loop
        new Thread(loop::run).start();
        
        // Post events
        loop.post(new Event() {
            public String getType() { return "USER_LOGIN"; }
            public Object getData() { return "john@example.com"; }
        });
        
        loop.postDelayed(new Event() {
            public String getType() { return "DATA_UPDATE"; }
            public Object getData() { return "New data available"; }
        }, 1, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
        
        // Let it run
        try {
            Thread.sleep(2000);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {}
        
        loop.stop();
    }
}
```

**When to Use Event Loop Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. In I/O-bound applications (web servers, proxies).

2. In GUI applications (event-driven UI).

3. When you want simplicity of single-threaded programming.

4. When you have many concurrent connections.

5. In JavaScript/Node.js environments (it's built-in).

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Node.js:** JavaScript runtime
- **Web Browsers:** JavaScript event loop
- **GUI Frameworks:** Swing, JavaFX event dispatch thread
- **Game Engines:** Update loops
- **Redis:** Single-threaded event-driven server

**Advantages of Event Loop Pattern**

1. **Simplicity:** Single thread, no concurrency issues.

2. **No Race Conditions:** No locks needed.

3. **Predictable:** Events processed in order.

4. **Lightweight:** No thread overhead.

5. **Good for I/O:** Handles many connections efficiently.

**Disadvantages of Event Loop Pattern**

1. **CPU-Bound Tasks:** Block the loop.

2. **Single Core Only:** Can't utilize multiple cores.

3. **Callback Hell:** Nested callbacks become complex.

4. **Error Handling:** Errors can crash the loop.

5. **No Preemption:** Long-running tasks starve others.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| Event Loop | Single-thread event processing | No parallelism |
| Reactor | Sync event demux | Can use thread pool |
| Proactor | Async event demux | OS async I/O |
| Actor | Message passing | Multiple actors |

**Event Loop vs Reactor**

- **Event Loop:** Usually single-threaded
- **Reactor:** Can use thread pool for handlers

**Event Loop vs Actor Model**

- **Event Loop:** Single queue, single thread
- **Actor Model:** Multiple actors, each with own queue

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Blocking Operations:** Never block the event loop.

2. **Too Many Callbacks:** Leads to callback hell.

3. **Error Propagation:** Errors must be caught.

4. **Zalgo Problem:** Mixing sync and async code.

5. **Memory Leaks:** Forgotten event listeners.

**Best Practices**

1. **Never Block:** Use async operations only.

2. **Keep Callbacks Small:** Break into smaller functions.

3. **Use Promises/Async-Await:** Avoid callback hell.

4. **Handle Errors:** Always have error handlers.

5. **Remove Listeners:** Clean up event listeners.

6. **Monitor Lag:** Watch for event loop lag.

7. **Use Worker Threads:** Offload CPU work.

---

[Page 29]

## Chapter 27: Publish-Subscribe Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Publish-Subscribe pattern decouples publishers (senders) from subscribers (receivers) through an intermediary message broker. Publishers emit messages without knowing who will receive them; subscribers express interest in certain messages without knowing who sent them.

**Understanding the Problem**

In distributed systems, direct communication between components creates tight coupling:

1. **Dependency:** Senders need to know receivers
2. **Scalability:** Hard to add new receivers
3. **Flexibility:** Changing receivers affects senders
4. **Reliability:** If receiver fails, sender may need to handle

**The Solution**

The Publish-Subscribe pattern introduces a message broker:

1. **Publishers:** Send messages to topics
2. **Subscribers:** Subscribe to topics of interest
3. **Broker:** Routes messages to all subscribers

**Key Concepts**

- **Publisher:** Produces messages
- **Subscriber:** Consumes messages
- **Topic/Channel:** Named destination for messages
- **Broker:** Routes messages to subscribers
- **Subscription:** Expression of interest in a topic

**Diagram: Publish-Subscribe**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   PUBLISH-SUBSCRIBE PATTERN                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                    PUBLISHERS                          │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐          │    │
│  │  │Publisher │  │Publisher │  │Publisher │          │    │
│  │  │    A     │  │    B     │  │    C     │          │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘          │    │
│  │       │             │             │                 │    │
│  │       │ publish     │ publish     │ publish         │    │
│  │       │ to Topic1   │ to Topic2   │ to Topic1       │    │
│  │       ▼             ▼             ▼                 │    │
│  └─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┘    │
│                        │                                    │
│                        ▼                                    │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                 MESSAGE BROKER                         │    │
│  │  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
│  │  │                    TOPICS                       │  │    │
│  │  │  ┌──────────┐        ┌──────────┐            │  │    │
│  │  │  │ Topic 1  │        │ Topic 2  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  │ ┌─────┐  │        │ ┌─────┐  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  │ │Msg A│  │        │ │Msg B│  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  │ │Msg C│  │        │ │Msg D│  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  │ └─────┘  │        │ └─────┘  │            │  │    │
│  │  │  └──────────┘        └──────────┘            │  │    │
│  │  └───────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
│  │                        │                            │    │
│  │                        │ routes to subscribers      │    │
│  │                        ▼                            │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                        │                                    │
│                        │                                    │
│        ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐                    │
│        │               │               │                    │
│        ▼               ▼               ▼                    │
│  ┌──────────┐    ┌──────────┐    ┌──────────┐              │
│  │Subscriber│    │Subscriber│    │Subscriber│              │
│  │    1     │    │    2     │    │    3     │              │
│  │(Topic1)  │    │(Topic1,2)│    │(Topic2)  │              │
│  └──────────┘    └──────────┘    └──────────┘              │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │              SUBSCRIPTION TYPES                        │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Point-to-Point (Queue):                            │    │
│  │    One message, one consumer                         │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Publish-Subscribe (Topic):                         │    │
│  │    One message, all subscribers                      │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Durable Subscription:                               │    │
│  │    Subscriber gets messages even when offline        │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Wildcard Subscription:                              │    │
│  │    Subscribe to patterns (Topic.*, Topic.#)         │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Implementation Example**

```java
import java.util.*;
import java.util.concurrent.*;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.*;

// 1. Message class
class Message {
    private final String topic;
    private final Object payload;
    private final long timestamp;
    
    public Message(String topic, Object payload) {
        this.topic = topic;
        this.payload = payload;
        this.timestamp = System.currentTimeMillis();
    }
    
    public String getTopic() { return topic; }
    public Object getPayload() { return payload; }
    public long getTimestamp() { return timestamp; }
}

// 2. Subscriber interface
interface Subscriber {
    void onMessage(Message message);
    String getId();
}

// 3. Simple In-Memory PubSub Broker
class PubSubBroker {
    private final Map<String, List<Subscriber>> topics = 
        new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final Map<String, BlockingQueue<Message>> topicQueues = 
        new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final ExecutorService executor = 
        Executors.newCachedThreadPool();
    
    public void subscribe(String topic, Subscriber subscriber) {
        topics.computeIfAbsent(topic, k -> new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>())
              .add(subscriber);
        System.out.println("Subscriber " + subscriber.getId() + 
            " subscribed to " + topic);
    }
    
    public void unsubscribe(String topic, Subscriber subscriber) {
        List<Subscriber> subscribers = topics.get(topic);
        if (subscribers != null) {
            subscribers.remove(subscriber);
            System.out.println("Subscriber " + subscriber.getId() + 
                " unsubscribed from " + topic);
        }
    }
    
    public void publish(String topic, Object payload) {
        Message message = new Message(topic, payload);
        publish(message);
    }
    
    public void publish(Message message) {
        List<Subscriber> subscribers = topics.get(message.getTopic());
        
        if (subscribers != null && !subscribers.isEmpty()) {
            for (Subscriber subscriber : subscribers) {
                // Deliver asynchronously
                executor.submit(() -> {
                    try {
                        subscriber.onMessage(message);
                    } catch (Exception e) {
                        System.err.println("Error delivering to " + 
                            subscriber.getId() + ": " + e.getMessage());
                    }
                });
            }
        } else {
            System.out.println("No subscribers for topic: " + 
                message.getTopic());
        }
    }
    
    public void shutdown() {
        executor.shutdown();
        try {
            if (!executor.awaitTermination(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS)) {
                executor.shutdownNow();
            }
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            executor.shutdownNow();
        }
    }
}

// 4. Example Subscribers
class LoggingSubscriber implements Subscriber {
    private final String id;
    
    public LoggingSubscriber(String id) {
        this.id = id;
    }
    
    @Override
    public void onMessage(Message message) {
        System.out.println("[" + id + "] Received: " + 
            message.getPayload() + " on topic " + message.getTopic());
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getId() { return id; }
}

class CountingSubscriber implements Subscriber {
    private final String id;
    private final AtomicInteger count = new AtomicInteger(0);
    
    public CountingSubscriber(String id) {
        this.id = id;
    }
    
    @Override
    public void onMessage(Message message) {
        int total = count.incrementAndGet();
        System.out.println("[" + id + "] Message count: " + total);
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getId() { return id; }
}

// 5. Pattern Matching Topic (Wildcard)
class PatternPubSub {
    private final Map<String, List<Subscriber>> exactTopics = 
        new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final List<PatternSubscription> patternSubscriptions = 
        new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    
    static class PatternSubscription {
        final String pattern;
        final Subscriber subscriber;
        
        PatternSubscription(String pattern, Subscriber subscriber) {
            this.pattern = pattern;
            this.subscriber = subscriber;
        }
        
        boolean matches(String topic) {
            // Simple wildcard: * matches one level, # matches multiple
            if (pattern.endsWith("#")) {
                String prefix = pattern.substring(0, pattern.length() - 1);
                return topic.startsWith(prefix);
            } else if (pattern.contains("*")) {
                // Simplified - in practice, use regex
                return topic.matches(pattern.replace("*", "[^.]+"));
            }
            return pattern.equals(topic);
        }
    }
    
    public void subscribe(String topic, Subscriber subscriber) {
        if (topic.contains("*") || topic.contains("#")) {
            patternSubscriptions.add(
                new PatternSubscription(topic, subscriber));
        } else {
            exactTopics.computeIfAbsent(topic, 
                k -> new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>()).add(subscriber);
        }
    }
    
    public void publish(String topic, Object payload) {
        // Send to exact matches
        List<Subscriber> exact = exactTopics.get(topic);
        if (exact != null) {
            for (Subscriber s : exact) {
                s.onMessage(new Message(topic, payload));
            }
        }
        
        // Send to pattern matches
        for (PatternSubscription ps : patternSubscriptions) {
            if (ps.matches(topic)) {
                ps.subscriber.onMessage(new Message(topic, payload));
            }
        }
    }
}

// 6. Usage Example
public class PubSubExample {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws InterruptedException {
        PubSubBroker broker = new PubSubBroker();
        
        // Create subscribers
        Subscriber logger1 = new LoggingSubscriber("Logger1");
        Subscriber logger2 = new LoggingSubscriber("Logger2");
        Subscriber counter = new CountingSubscriber("Counter");
        
        // Subscribe to topics
        broker.subscribe("user.login", logger1);
        broker.subscribe("user.logout", logger1);
        broker.subscribe("user.login", logger2);
        broker.subscribe("system.status", counter);
        broker.subscribe("user.*", counter); // Wildcard example
        
        // Publish messages
        broker.publish("user.login", "User john logged in");
        broker.publish("user.logout", "User john logged out");
        broker.publish("system.status", "All systems operational");
        broker.publish("database.error", "Connection failed");
        
        // Wait for async delivery
        Thread.sleep(1000);
        
        broker.shutdown();
    }
}
```

**Distributed PubSub with Message Queue**

```java
// Simplified JMS-like interface
interface MessageQueue {
    void publish(String topic, Message message);
    void subscribe(String topic, MessageListener listener);
}

interface MessageListener {
    void onMessage(Message message);
}

class DistributedPubSub implements MessageQueue {
    private final Map<String, List<MessageListener>> listeners = 
        new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final ExecutorService executor = 
        Executors.newFixedThreadPool(10);
    private final String brokerId;
    
    public DistributedPubSub(String brokerId) {
        this.brokerId = brokerId;
    }
    
    @Override
    public void publish(String topic, Message message) {
        // In distributed system, this would publish to a cluster
        executor.submit(() -> {
            List<MessageListener> topicListeners = listeners.get(topic);
            if (topicListeners != null) {
                for (MessageListener listener : topicListeners) {
                    listener.onMessage(message);
                }
            }
        });
    }
    
    @Override
    public void subscribe(String topic, MessageListener listener) {
        listeners.computeIfAbsent(topic, 
            k -> new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>()).add(listener);
    }
    
    // Cluster coordination
    public void connectToBroker(String remoteBroker) {
        // In real implementation, establish connection
        System.out.println("Connected to remote broker: " + remoteBroker);
    }
}
```

**When to Use Publish-Subscribe Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. When you need to decouple message producers from consumers.

2. When multiple consumers need to receive the same messages.

3. When you don't know who will need the messages.

4. When the system needs to be extensible (add new consumers easily).

5. In event-driven architectures.

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Message Queues:** RabbitMQ, Apache Kafka, ActiveMQ
- **Event-Driven Microservices:** Service communication
- **Notification Systems:** Push notifications
- **Log Aggregation:** Centralized logging
- **Real-Time Data Feeds:** Stock tickers, news feeds

**Advantages of Publish-Subscribe Pattern**

1. **Loose Coupling:** Publishers and subscribers don't know each other.

2. **Scalability:** Can add many subscribers.

3. **Flexibility:** Subscribers can come and go.

4. **Asynchronous:** Publishers don't wait for subscribers.

5. **Multiple Receivers:** One message, many subscribers.

**Disadvantages of Publish-Subscribe Pattern**

1. **Complexity:** Broker adds complexity.

2. **Delivery Guarantees:** At-most-once, at-least-once, exactly-once.

3. **Ordering:** Messages may arrive out of order.

4. **Broker Bottleneck:** Broker can become bottleneck.

5. **Debugging Difficulty:** Harder to trace message flow.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| Pub-Sub | Many-to-many | Broker-mediated |
| Observer | One-to-many | Subject knows observers |
| Producer-Consumer | Point-to-point | One consumer per message |
| Message Queue | Queue-based | Usually point-to-point |

**Pub-Sub vs Observer**

- **Pub-Sub:** Publishers don't know subscribers
- **Observer:** Subject knows about observers

**Pub-Sub vs Message Queue**

- **Pub-Sub:** Each message to all subscribers
- **Message Queue:** Each message to one consumer

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Message Loss:** No persistence, subscribers offline.

2. **Ordering Issues:** Messages arrive out of order.

3. **Broker Failure:** Single point of failure.

4. **Performance Bottleneck:** Broker can't keep up.

5. **Debugging Complexity:** Hard to trace messages.

**Best Practices**

1. **Use Durable Subscriptions:** For important messages.

2. **Implement Retry Logic:** Handle temporary failures.

3. **Monitor Queue Depth:** Alert on backlog.

4. **Design for Idempotency:** Handle duplicate messages.

5. **Use Message Schemas:** Ensure compatibility.

6. **Consider Partitioning:** For high throughput.

7. **Implement Dead Letter Queues:** For failed messages.

---

[Page 30]

## Chapter 28: Half-Sync / Half-Async Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Half-Sync/Half-Async pattern decouples synchronous and asynchronous processing in concurrent systems. It uses two layers: an asynchronous layer for I/O and a synchronous layer for processing, with a queue between them.

**Understanding the Problem**

Many systems have both asynchronous I/O requirements and synchronous processing needs:

1. **Asynchronous I/O:** Efficient for many connections
2. **Synchronous Processing:** Simpler for business logic
3. **Integration:** Need to bridge the two models

**The Solution**

The Half-Sync/Half-Async pattern provides:

1. **Async Layer:** Handles I/O asynchronously
2. **Queue:** Buffers requests between layers
3. **Sync Layer:** Processes requests synchronously in thread pool
4. **Service Layer:** Interface to applications

**Key Concepts**

- **Async Layer:** Manages I/O, collects requests
- **Queue:** Holds pending requests
- **Sync Layer:** Thread pool processing requests
- **Demultiplexer:** Dispatches I/O events
- **Dispatcher:** Routes completed requests

**Diagram: Half-Sync/Half-Async**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  HALF-SYNC / HALF-ASYNC                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │               ASYNCHRONOUS LAYER                       │    │
│  │  (Handles I/O, event demultiplexing)                  │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                      │    │
│  │  │  Reactor │                                      │    │
│  │  │  or      │                                      │    │
│  │  │  Proactor│                                      │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                      │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       │ receives requests                          │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │           QUEUE LAYER                       │   │    │
│  │  │  (Buffer between async and sync)            │   │    │
│  │  │  ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐          │   │    │
│  │  │  │Req 1│ │Req 2│ │Req 3│ │Req 4│          │   │    │
│  │  │  └─────┘ └─────┘ └─────┘ └─────┘          │   │    │
│  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │    │
│  │       │                                            │    │
│  │       │ worker threads take requests               │    │
│  │       ▼                                            │    │
│  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │            SYNCHRONOUS LAYER                 │   │    │
│  │  │  (Thread pool, blocking operations)         │   │    │
│  │  │                                             │   │    │
│  │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  │   │    │
│  │  │  │ Worker 1 │  │ Worker 2 │  │ Worker 3 │  │   │    │
│  │  │  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  │   │    │
│  │  │       │             │             │        │   │    │
│  │  │       │ process     │ process     │ process │   │    │
│  │  │       ▼             ▼             ▼        │   │    │
│  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                  SEQUENCE DIAGRAM                      │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Async    Queue    Worker1    Worker2    Sync      │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │        │         │    │
│  │   │─request─>│         │         │        │         │    │
│  │   │        │─enqueue─>│         │        │         │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │        │         │    │
│  │   │─request─>│─enqueue─┼────────>│        │         │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │        │         │    │
│  │   │        │<─dequeue─│         │        │         │    │
│  │   │        │         │─process─>│        │         │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │        │         │    │
│  │   │        │<─dequeue─┼─────────│        │         │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │─process─>│         │    │
│  │   │        │         │         │        │         │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │               EXAMPLE: WEB SERVER                      │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Async Layer:                                       │    │
│  │    • Accepts connections                            │    │
│  │    • Reads requests                                 │    │
│  │    • Places requests in queue                       │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Queue Layer:                                       │    │
│  │    • Bounded buffer                                │    │
│  │    • Provides backpressure                          │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  Sync Layer:                                        │    │
│  │    • Processes requests (may block on DB)          │    │
│  │    • Generates responses                            │    │
│  │    • Sends responses (or via async layer)          │    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Implementation Example**

```java
import java.util.concurrent.*;
import java.util.*;

// 1. Request/Response classes
class Request {
    private final String id;
    private final String data;
    private final CompletableFuture<Response> future = new CompletableFuture<>();
    
    public Request(String id, String data) {
        this.id = id;
        this.data = data;
    }
    
    public String getId() { return id; }
    public String getData() { return data; }
    
    public void complete(Response response) {
        future.complete(response);
    }
    
    public Response getResponse() throws ExecutionException, InterruptedException {
        return future.get();
    }
}

class Response {
    private final String requestId;
    private final String result;
    
    public Response(String requestId, String result) {
        this.requestId = requestId;
        this.result = result;
    }
    
    public String getRequestId() { return requestId; }
    public String getResult() { return result; }
}

// 2. Async Layer (Reactor-like)
class AsyncLayer {
    private final BlockingQueue<Request> requestQueue;
    private volatile boolean running = true;
    
    public AsyncLayer(BlockingQueue<Request> queue) {
        this.requestQueue = queue;
    }
    
    public void start() {
        // Simulate receiving requests
        Thread acceptor = new Thread(() -> {
            int requestId = 0;
            while (running) {
                try {
                    // Simulate incoming request
                    Request req = new Request("req-" + requestId++, 
                        "Data-" + requestId);
                    System.out.println("Async: Received " + req.getId());
                    requestQueue.put(req);
                    
                    Thread.sleep(100); // Simulate request arrival rate
                } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                    break;
                }
            }
        });
        acceptor.setDaemon(true);
        acceptor.start();
    }
    
    public void stop() {
        running = false;
    }
}

// 3. Synchronous Layer (Thread Pool)
class SyncLayer {
    private final ExecutorService executor;
    private final BlockingQueue<Request> requestQueue;
    private volatile boolean running = true;
    
    public SyncLayer(int threadCount, BlockingQueue<Request> queue) {
        this.executor = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(threadCount);
        this.requestQueue = queue;
    }
    
    public void start() {
        for (int i = 0; i < Runtime.getRuntime().availableProcessors(); i++) {
            executor.submit(() -> {
                while (running) {
                    try {
                        // Take request from queue (blocks if empty)
                        Request request = requestQueue.take();
                        
                        // Process request synchronously (may block)
                        System.out.println("Sync: Processing " + 
                            request.getId() + " on " + 
                            Thread.currentThread().getName());
                        
                        // Simulate processing (could be database, computation)
                        Thread.sleep(500);
                        
                        // Create response
                        Response response = new Response(
                            request.getId(), 
                            "Processed: " + request.getData()
                        );
                        
                        // Complete the request's future
                        request.complete(response);
                        
                        System.out.println("Sync: Completed " + 
                            request.getId());
                        
                    } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                        Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                        break;
                    }
                }
            });
        }
    }
    
    public void stop() {
        running = false;
        executor.shutdown();
        try {
            executor.awaitTermination(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            executor.shutdownNow();
        }
    }
}

// 4. Response Handler (could be async or sync)
class ResponseHandler {
    public void handleResponse(Response response) {
        System.out.println("Response for " + response.getRequestId() + 
            ": " + response.getResult());
    }
}

// 5. Complete Half-Sync/Half-Async System
class HalfSyncHalfAsyncSystem {
    private final BlockingQueue<Request> queue = 
        new LinkedBlockingQueue<>(100); // Bounded queue
    private final AsyncLayer asyncLayer;
    private final SyncLayer syncLayer;
    private final ResponseHandler responseHandler;
    
    public HalfSyncHalfAsyncSystem(int threadCount) {
        this.asyncLayer = new AsyncLayer(queue);
        this.syncLayer = new SyncLayer(threadCount, queue);
        this.responseHandler = new ResponseHandler();
    }
    
    public void start() {
        asyncLayer.start();
        syncLayer.start();
        
        // Start response processing thread
        new Thread(() -> {
            while (true) {
                // In real system, responses would be collected differently
                try {
                    Thread.sleep(1000);
                } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                    break;
                }
            }
        }).start();
    }
    
    public void stop() {
        asyncLayer.stop();
        syncLayer.stop();
    }
    
    // Client code to submit requests and wait for responses
    public CompletableFuture<Response> submitRequest(String data) {
        Request request = new Request(UUID.randomUUID().toString(), data);
        try {
            queue.put(request);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            CompletableFuture<Response> failed = new CompletableFuture<>();
            failed.completeExceptionally(e);
            return failed;
        }
        return request.future;
    }
    
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        HalfSyncHalfAsyncSystem system = new HalfSyncHalfAsyncSystem(4);
        system.start();
        
        // Submit some requests and wait for responses
        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
            final int id = i;
            system.submitRequest("Data-" + i)
                .thenAccept(response -> {
                    System.out.println("Got response for " + 
                        response.getRequestId());
                });
        }
        
        // Let system run
        Thread.sleep(10000);
        system.stop();
    }
}
```

**When to Use Half-Sync/Half-Async Pattern**

**Suitable Situations:**

1. When you have both I/O-intensive and CPU-intensive operations.

2. When you want to separate concerns (I/O from processing).

3. When you need to scale I/O and processing independently.

4. In high-performance network servers.

5. When you need to use blocking APIs in an asynchronous system.

**Real-World Use Cases:**

- **Web Servers:** Accept connections async, process requests in thread pool
- **Database Drivers:** Async network, sync query execution
- **Gateway Systems:** Async I/O, sync processing
- **API Gateways:** Async request handling, sync service calls

**Advantages of Half-Sync/Half-Async Pattern**

1. **Separation of Concerns:** I/O and processing layers decoupled.

2. **Independent Scaling:** Each layer can be scaled separately.

3. **Simplicity:** Processing layer can use blocking APIs.

4. **Performance:** Combines async I/O efficiency with sync processing simplicity.

5. **Flexibility:** Can use different threading models per layer.

**Disadvantages of Half-Sync/Half-Async Pattern**

1. **Queue Management:** Queue sizing and overflow handling.

2. **Backpressure:** Must handle when queue fills.

3. **Complexity:** More complex than pure sync or async.

4. **Latency:** Queue adds latency.

5. **Debugging:** Harder to trace requests across layers.

**Comparison with Other Patterns**

| Pattern | Purpose | Difference |
|---------|---------|------------|
| Half-Sync/Half-Async | Bridge sync/async | Two-layer |
| Reactor | Sync event demux | Single layer |
| Proactor | Async event demux | Single layer |
| Leader-Followers | Thread pool | Different model |

**Half-Sync/Half-Async vs Reactor**

- **Half-Sync/Half-Async:** Has processing layer separate from I/O
- **Reactor:** Handles processing in same thread or dispatches

**Half-Sync/Half-Async vs Thread Pool**

- **Half-Sync/Half-Async:** Has async I/O layer
- **Thread Pool:** May not have dedicated I/O layer

**Common Pitfalls**

1. **Queue Overflow:** Unbounded queues cause memory issues.

2. **Backpressure Not Implemented:** Producers overwhelm queue.

3. **Thread Pool Sizing:** Too few threads cause queuing.

4. **Response Path:** Sending responses back may need async handling.

5. **Error Propagation:** Errors across layers must be handled.

**Best Practices**

1. **Use Bounded Queue:** Prevent memory exhaustion.

2. **Implement Backpressure:** Slow producers when queue full.

3. **Monitor Queue Size:** Alert on high queue depth.

4. **Size Thread Pool Appropriately:** Based on workload.

5. **Handle Shutdown Gracefully:** Process remaining requests.

6. **Consider Response Path:** May need async return path.

---

[Page 31]

# PART SIX: ADVANCED PATTERNS

## Chapter 29: Balking Pattern

**Detailed Explanation**

The Balking pattern executes an operation only if the object is in an appropriate state. If not, it returns immediately without waiting. This pattern is useful when waiting would be pointless or would cause problems.

**Understanding the Problem**

Sometimes an operation should only be performed if certain conditions are met:

- Starting a service that's already started
- Saving data that hasn't changed
- Processing an event when already processing
- Updating a configuration that's locked

If the condition isn't met, waiting might be wasteful or dangerous.

**The Solution**

The Balking pattern checks the state before executing. If the state is invalid, the method returns immediately (or throws an exception). This is like a "guard clause" for concurrency.

**Key Concepts**

- **Guard:** Condition that must be true to proceed
- **Balk:** Return immediately when guard fails
- **State Check:** Check condition without blocking
- **Atomic Check:** Must be done atomically with state change

**Diagram: Balking Pattern**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      BALKING PATTERN                          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │                 OPERATION FLOW                         │    │
│  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    │
│  │                                                     │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐                                       │    │
│  │  │  Caller  │                                       │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘                                       │    │
│  │       │                                             │    │
│  │       │ request operation                           │    │
│  │       ▼                                             │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │    │
│  │  │            synchronized block                │   │    │
│  │  │                                             │   │    │
```
